Produce a precise, code-cited analysis for a user who is walking the algorithm step-by-step to then
IMPROVE de-novo (annotation-free, p00) quality. Cover:

1. MECHANISM: what exactly does discover_candidates produce? Trace both paths:
   (a) GTF-passthrough candidates (when annotation present) — how an annotated transcript becomes a
       candidate, source tag, ids.
   (b) NOVEL de-novo candidates — the DEFAULT path. Is it the chain-cluster path
       (config.chain_cluster_discovery) or a legacy per-read path? What is the default value of
       chain_cluster_discovery? Trace: read CIGAR -> IntronChain (exact coords?), grouping key
       (exact intron chain? 3' handling?), folding (exact sub-chain fold, mono-fold, span-guard),
       wobble/cassette/containment clustering, what becomes one candidate, id assignment, and how
       supporting_read_ids / read_spans / read_sequences are populated.

2. DEFAULTS: which generation features are ON by default vs behind flags (chain_cluster_discovery,
   chain_cluster_wobble_bp, chain_cluster_fold_monoexon, chain_cluster_fold_span_guard,
   chain_cluster_cassette_max_exon_bp, min_novel_reads, three_prime_threshold, denovo_graph*). Give
   the actual default values from config.py.

3. SOUNDNESS / CORRECTNESS: is the generation logic sound? Any bug, edge case, non-determinism, or
   leak (e.g. reads double-counted, candidates on wrong strand, empty-chain handling, off-by-one in
   intron extraction)?

4. THE DE-NOVO QUALITY ROOT CAUSE: it is believed p00 structural precision (~65% vs isoquant ~91%)
   stems from EXACT IntronChain equality — a minimap-wobbled junction (1000,2000) vs (1002,1998)
   becomes TWO different candidates, so wobble shadows proliferate. Confirm or refute from the code:
   is intron-chain grouping/collapse EXACT-coordinate, and does the wobble/cassette clustering merge
   them or keep both as separate candidates? Where precisely does a wobble shadow survive as its own
   candidate?

5. TUNING TARGETS: for the perf phase, what in THIS step is the highest-leverage thing to change to
   raise de-novo precision without losing recall, and what are the risks (esp. the read-reassignment
   trap: dropping/merging shadows post-hoc reshuffles their reads).

Be concrete with file:line cites. Do not modify anything. This is analysis for a decision, keep it
structured but complete.

fin/cli.py
48:@click.option("--chain-cluster-discovery/--no-chain-cluster-discovery", "chain_cluster_discovery", default=True, show_default=True, help="PRODUCTION DEFAULT generation. Group reads by intron chain (3' IGNORED), collapse EXACT sub-chains, cluster wobble/cassette/containment keeping members; NO canonical-search shadows. Replaces the legacy 3'+exact-chain grouping + canonical expansion. p00 no-gates: raw candidates 175k->37k, c -73%, j -88%, structural precision 10->26%, at ~20% distinct-truth recall cost (92% from the exact sub-chain collapse). --no- reverts to the legacy discovery.")
51:@click.option("--chain-cluster-fold-monoexon/--no-chain-cluster-fold-monoexon", "chain_cluster_fold_monoexon", default=True, show_default=True, help="(--chain-cluster-discovery) PRODUCTION DEFAULT. Fold a single-exon read whose aligned span lies wholly inside one exon of a multi-exon candidate INTO that candidate (a 5'/3' degradation fragment) instead of emitting a standalone single-exon candidate. Reads inside an intron (a different gene) or contained in no multi-exon candidate stay separate. Suppresses single-exon truncation FPs (p00 m2_em: out -400, `=` +91, Pr 66.0->69.9). --no- reverts to standalone mono candidates.")
52:@click.option("--chain-cluster-fold-span-guard/--no-chain-cluster-fold-span-guard", "chain_cluster_fold_span_guard", default=True, show_default=True, help="(--chain-cluster-discovery) PRODUCTION DEFAULT. Only fold a read into an exact-sub-chain container if its aligned span does NOT run exonically across one of the container's EXTRA introns. A read that spans such an intron (retained-intron / alternative isoform) contradicts it and is kept as its own candidate instead of being absorbed. --no- reverts to the unconditional exact-sub-chain fold.")
88:@click.option("--denovo-graph/--no-denovo-graph", "denovo_graph", default=False, show_default=True, help="(EXPERIMENT) De-novo intron-graph assembly: pool all reads' junctions, cluster wobbles to a read-count consensus (--denovo-graph-tol), build a read-adjacency graph, and EXTEND each read's chain through UNAMBIGUOUSLY-supported edges (>= --denovo-graph-min-edge-reads) into a maximal full-length chain — assembling truncated 3'-biased dRNA reads into full transcripts (attacks the measured #1 de-novo error: 'c' contained/truncated). Stops at genuine branch points (no fabricated combos). Reads grouped by extended chain; candidate span = union of its reads' extents, sequence from genome. NOT annotation-snap. Default OFF (byte-identical).")
91:@click.option("--denovo-graph-tss-brake/--no-denovo-graph-tss-brake", "denovo_graph_tss_brake", default=True, show_default=True, help="(--denovo-graph) 5'-TSS brake: don't extend a chain 5'-ward past a real transcription start. dRNA truncation is 5'-ward, so a truncated read of a LONG transcript stops at a random 5' position while a COMPLETE read of a genuine short isoform stops at its real TSS. If >= --denovo-graph-tss-frac of the reads starting at a chain's 5' junction pile their 5'-end within --denovo-graph-tss-tol bp (and >= --denovo-graph-tss-min-reads), that's a real TSS and the short isoform is kept instead of merged into the longer one. Validated on p00: real short-isoform TSS carry 40-90% of read-5'-ends; degradation scatters. --no- disables (pure assembly, over-merges).")
104:    help="Quantification engine. 'm2_em' (production default): EM seeded by the PURE tie-break junction-NLL M2 distance (M1/AS selects each read's best-AS tie set + mappability mask; per-event junction NLL is the sole graded distance over that tie set), no M3 unless --m3-coherence. 'argmax': mappy AS argmax + M2 krill junction tiebreak, hard counts, no EM. 'm1_em': EM seeded by M1 mappy distance (beta=0). 'cluster': assign reads to candidates WITHIN each generation cluster only (CandidateSet.clusters), M1 within-cluster + summed-LLR M2 tiebreak + main-peak containment (fin.pipeline.cluster_quant). All signal scoring is in-memory krill.",

    25	def _generate_novel_id() -> str:
    26	    """Generate a short unique ID for a novel candidate."""
    27	    import uuid
    28	
    29	    return "novel_" + uuid.uuid4().hex[:8]

    32	def _intron_chains_match(a: IntronChain, b: IntronChain) -> bool:
    33	    """Check if two intron chains are identical."""
    34	    return a.introns == b.introns
    35	
    36	
    37	def _spatial_read_clusters(read_ids, spans):
    38	    """Group chain-less (single-exon) reads into non-overlapping genomic loci via
    39	    single-linkage over span overlap. Without this, every mono read in an interval
    40	    pools into ONE candidate whose union span bridges distinct single-exon genes.
    41	    Reads absent from ``spans`` are dropped. Returns a list of read-id lists."""
    42	    items = sorted((spans[r][0], spans[r][1], r) for r in read_ids if r in spans)
    43	    clusters = []
    44	    cur = []
    45	    cur_end = None
    46	    for s, e, r in items:
    47	        if cur and s > cur_end:                 # no overlap with the current locus
    48	            clusters.append([x[2] for x in cur])
    49	            cur = []
    50	            cur_end = None
    51	        cur.append((s, e, r))
    52	        cur_end = e if cur_end is None else max(cur_end, e)
    53	    if cur:
    54	        clusters.append([x[2] for x in cur])
    55	    return clusters
    56	
    57	
    58	def _best_overlap_gtf(mono_gtf, lo: int, hi: int):
    59	    """Pick the single-exon GTF candidate whose span most overlaps ``[lo, hi)``.
    60	
    61	    Mono (empty-chain) candidates must NOT be matched to GTF by chain equality -- every
    62	    single-exon transcript shares the empty chain, so a dict keyed by chain collapses
    63	    them and routes all single-exon reads to whichever mono GTF was inserted first.
    64	    Match by genomic overlap instead; returns None when nothing overlaps."""
    65	    best = None
    66	    best_ov = 0
    67	    for gc, gs, ge in mono_gtf:
    68	        ov = min(hi, ge) - max(lo, gs)
    69	        if ov > best_ov:
    70	            best_ov = ov
    71	            best = gc
    72	    return best
    73	
    74	
    75	_COMPLEMENT = str.maketrans("ACGTNacgtn", "TGCANtgcan")
    76	
    77	
    78	def _reverse_complement(seq: str) -> str:
    79	    return seq.translate(_COMPLEMENT)[::-1]
    80	
    81	
    82	def _exons_from_chain(start: int, end: int, chain: IntronChain) -> List[tuple]:
    83	    """Derive exon (start, end) blocks from an intron chain spanning [start, end)."""
    84	    introns = chain.introns
    85	    if not introns:
    86	        return [(start, end)]
    87	    exons = [(start, introns[0][0])]
    88	    for i in range(len(introns) - 1):
    89	        exons.append((introns[i][1], introns[i + 1][0]))
    90	    exons.append((introns[-1][1], end))
    91	    # Drop any zero-length blocks defensively
    92	    return [(s, e) for s, e in exons if e > s]
    93	
    94	
    95	def _build_spliced_sequence(
    96	    genome_fasta: str,
    97	    start: int,
    98	    end: int,
    99	    chain: IntronChain,
   100	    strand: str,
   101	) -> str:
   102	    """Stitch a spliced transcript sequence from a genome chromosome string.
   103	
   104	    Args:
   105	        genome_fasta: Full chromosome sequence string.
   106	        start: 0-based transcript start (left edge of first exon).
   107	        end: 0-based exclusive transcript end (right edge of last exon).
   108	        chain: Intron chain in genomic coordinates.
   109	        strand: '+' or '-'. Reverse complement is applied for '-'.
   110	
   111	    Returns:
   112	        Spliced cDNA sequence, or empty string when bounds are unusable.
   113	    """
   114	    if not genome_fasta or end <= start:
   115	        return ""
   116	    chrom_len = len(genome_fasta)
   117	    # Clip to chromosome bounds.
   118	    s = max(0, min(start, chrom_len))
   119	    e = max(0, min(end, chrom_len))
   120	    if e <= s:
   121	        return ""
   122	    parts = []
   123	    for ex_s, ex_e in _exons_from_chain(s, e, chain):
   124	        ex_s_c = max(s, min(ex_s, chrom_len))
   125	        ex_e_c = max(s, min(ex_e, chrom_len))
   126	        if ex_e_c > ex_s_c:
   127	            parts.append(genome_fasta[ex_s_c:ex_e_c])
   128	    seq = "".join(parts)
   129	    if strand == "-":
   130	        seq = _reverse_complement(seq)
   131	    return seq
   132	
   133	
   134	def _three_prime_within_threshold(pos_a: int, pos_b: int, threshold: int) -> bool:
   135	    return abs(pos_a - pos_b) <= threshold
   136	
   137	
   138	def _graph_groups(non_fusion_reads, strand, tol, min_edge_reads,
   139	                  tss_brake=True, tss_tol=20, tss_min_reads=3, tss_frac=0.4):
   140	    """Build novel-candidate read groups via intron-graph assembly: extend each
   141	    read's chain to a maximal read-supported chain (:func:`assemble_chains`),
   142	    group reads by that extended chain, and key each group by (TES, IntronChain)
   143	    for the shared candidate-creation path. TES = the group's 3' terminus (max
   144	    reference_end on '+', min reference_start on '-'). Truncated reads on the same
   145	    maximal path land in ONE group -> one full-length candidate.
   146	
   147	    ``tss_brake`` passes each read's 5'-end position to the assembler so genuine
   148	    short isoforms sitting at a real TSS peak are not extended (merged) away."""
   149	    from fin.candidates.junction_graph import assemble_chains
   150	
   151	    reads_kept = []
   152	    chains = []
   153	    five_prime_pos = []
   154	    for rd in non_fusion_reads:
   155	        cigar = rd.get("cigartuples")
   156	        ref_start = rd.get("reference_start")
   157	        if cigar is None or ref_start is None:
   158	            continue
   159	        reads_kept.append(rd)
   160	        chains.append(tuple(extract_intron_chain(cigar, ref_start).introns))
   161	        # read 5'-end = the truncation-prone terminus (strand-aware)
   162	        five_prime_pos.append(rd.get("reference_end") if strand == "-" else ref_start)
   163	    if not chains:
   164	        return {}
   165	    _, extended = assemble_chains(
   166	        chains, tol, min_edge_reads,
   167	        five_prime_pos=(five_prime_pos if tss_brake else None),
   168	        strand=strand, tss_tol=tss_tol,
   169	        min_tss_reads=tss_min_reads, tss_frac=tss_frac,
   170	    )
   171	
   172	    by_chain: dict = {}
   173	    for i, rd in enumerate(reads_kept):
   174	        by_chain.setdefault(extended[i], []).append(rd)
   175	
   176	    groups: dict = {}
   177	    for ext_chain, reads in by_chain.items():
   178	        if strand == "-":
   179	            tes = min((rd.get("reference_start", 0) for rd in reads), default=0)
   180	        else:
   181	            tes = max((rd.get("reference_end", 0) for rd in reads), default=0)
   182	        groups[(tes, IntronChain(introns=ext_chain))] = reads
   183	    return groups
   184	
   185	
   186	def _chain_cluster_candidates(
   187	    interval, non_fusion_reads, all_read_ids, read_sequences, gtf_reader,
   188	    genome_fasta, strand, min_novel_reads, *, wobble_bp, cassette_max_exon_bp,
   189	    fold_monoexon_contained=False, fold_span_guard=False,
   190	) -> CandidateSet:
   191	    """NEW generation: build candidates via intron-chain clustering.
   192	
   193	    Group reads by intron chain (3' ignored), fold EXACT sub-chains, cluster
   194	    wobble/cassette/containment (members kept); each surviving member chain is a NOVEL
   195	    candidate whose span is the UNION of its reads' extents and whose sequence is
   196	    stitched from the genome. A novel chain that EXACTLY matches a GTF transcript's
   197	    chain merges its reads into that GTF candidate instead. No canonical search.
   198	    """
   199	    from fin.candidates.chain_cluster import cluster_read_chains
   200	
   201	    read_chains = []
   202	    spans: dict = {}
   203	    for rd in non_fusion_reads:
   204	        cigar = rd.get("cigartuples")
   205	        ref_start = rd.get("reference_start")
   206	        if cigar is None or ref_start is None:
   207	            continue
   208	        read_chains.append((rd, extract_intron_chain(cigar, ref_start)))
   209	        q = rd.get("query_name")
   210	        if q is not None:
   211	            spans[q] = (ref_start, rd.get("reference_end", ref_start))
   212	
   213	    clusters = cluster_read_chains(
   214	        read_chains, wobble_bp=wobble_bp, cassette_max_exon_bp=cassette_max_exon_bp,
   215	        fold_monoexon_contained=fold_monoexon_contained,
   216	        fold_span_guard=fold_span_guard)
   217	
   218	    # GTF candidates (identical to the legacy path).
   219	    gtf_candidates: List[TranscriptCandidate] = []
   220	    gtf_by_chain: dict = {}
   221	    if gtf_reader is not None:
   222	        for tx in gtf_reader.get_transcripts_in_region(
   223	                interval.chrom, interval.start, interval.end):
   224	            # Interval is strand-separated (reads are strand-filtered into it), so an
   225	            # opposite-strand annotation must NOT enter and compete for reads / absorb
   226	            # same-chain support. (No effect when the interval is unstranded or there is
   227	            # no GTF, e.g. p00.)
   228	            if interval.strand is not None and tx.strand != interval.strand:
   229   230	            chain = gtf_transcript_to_intron_chain(tx)
   231	            tx.sort_features()
   232	            three_prime = tx.end if tx.strand == "+" else tx.start
   233	            gc = TranscriptCandidate(
   234	                candidate_id=tx.transcript_id, intron_chain=chain,
   235	                three_prime_pos=three_prime, sequence=tx.get_spliced_sequence(genome_fasta),
            gtf_candidates.append(gc)
   239	            gtf_by_chain.setdefault(chain, gc)   # first GTF wins on an exact-chain tie
   240	
   243	    mono_gtf = [(gc, gc.start, gc.end)
   244	                for gc in gtf_candidates if not gc.intron_chain.introns]
   246	   247	    # gates (canonical_gate, junction-dominance, fusion) drop/reorder candidate_set
   248	    # candidates after discovery, so an id survives those mutations where an index
   249	    # would go stale. A member with no candidate (empty span/seq) contributes nothing.
   250	    candidates: List[TranscriptCandidate] = list(gtf_candidates)
   252	    # container candidate_id -> its folded SHADOW sub-chains (for 5'-TSS recovery).
   253	    # Each shadow: (intron_chain_tuple, sorted_read_id_tuple). GTF ids can recur   255	    shadow_map: Dict[str, List[Tuple[Tuple[Tuple[int, int], ...], Tuple[str, ...]]]] = {}
   256	    def _emit(chain, member_reads, start, end, gc):
   257	        """Merge ``member_reads`` into GTF ``gc`` (if any) else emit a novel candidate.
   258	        Returns the candidate_id, or None when the spliced sequence cannot be built."""
   259	   260	            gc.supporting_read_ids.update(member_reads)
   261	            return gc.candidate_id
   262	        seq = _build_spliced_sequence(genome_fasta, start, end, chain, strand)
   263	        if not seq:
   264	            return None
   265	        cid = _generate_novel_id()
   266	        candidates.append(TranscriptCandidate(
   267	            candidate_id=cid, intron_chain=chain,
   268	            three_prime_pos=(end if strand == "+" else start), sequence=seq,
   269	            source="novel", supporting_read_ids=set(member_reads),
   270	            chrom=interval.chrom, strand=strand, start=start, end=end))
   271	        return cid
   274	        ids: List[str] = []
   275	        for m in cl.members:
   277	                starts = [spans[r][0] for r in m.read_ids if r in spans]
   278	                ends = [spans[r][1] for r in m.read_ids if r in spans]
   279	                if not starts:
   280	                    continue
   281	                cand_id = _emit(m.chain, m.read_ids, min(starts), max(ends),
	                if cand_id is None:
   284	                    continue
   285                # (5'-TSS recovery). Mono members carry none.
   288	                member_shadows = [
   289	                    (tuple(f.chain.introns), tuple(sorted(f.read_ids)))
   292	                if member_shadows:
   295	                # mono (single-exon): split the pooled chain-less reads into per-locus
   296	                # groups so distinct single-exon genes are not bridged, then overlap-match
   297	   298	                for group in _spatial_read_clusters(m.read_ids, spans):
   299	                    gs = [spans[r][0] for r in group]
   300	                    ge = [spans[r][1] for r in group]
   301	                    lo, hi = min(gs), max(ge)
   305	                        ids.append(cand_id)
   306	        cluster_id_lists.append(ids)
   307	
   310	        candidates = [c for c in candidates
   311	                      if c.source != "novel"
   312	                      or len(c.supporting_read_ids) >= min_novel_reads]
   313	        keep_ids = {c.candidate_id for c in candidates}
   314	        cluster_id_lists = [
   315	            [cid for cid in ids if cid in keep_ids] for ids in cluster_id_lists
   316	        ]
   317	        # Prune shadow entries whose container candidate was dropped.
   318	        shadow_map = {cid: sh for cid, sh in shadow_map.items() if cid in keep_ids}
   319	
   320	    logger.info(
   321	        "Interval %s: %d candidates (%d GTF, %d novel) from %d reads [chain-cluster]",
   322	        interval.region_string, len(candidates),
   323	        sum(1 for c in candidates if c.source == "gtf"),
   324	        sum(1 for c in candidates if c.source == "novel"), len(all_read_ids))
   325	
   326	    return CandidateSet(
   327	        interval=interval, candidates=candidates, read_ids=all_read_ids,
   328	        read_sequences=read_sequences, clusters=cluster_id_lists,
   329	        shadows=shadow_map, read_spans=spans)
   330

   332	def discover_candidates(
   333	    interval: GenomicInterval,
   336	    genome_fasta: str,

   341	    canonical_motifs: tuple = ("GT-AG", "GC-AG", "AT-AC"),
   342	    denovo_wobble_tol: int = 0,

   373	   374	            exceeds the cap fall back to their original chain.

   397	            # corrupting strand assignment (a candidate emitted on the wrong
   398	            # strand can never match) and read-to-candidate quantification.

   406	                # Capture primary-alignment sequence only — scope is this

   423	        )

   438	            fold_monoexon_contained=chain_cluster_fold_monoexon,
   439	            fold_span_guard=chain_cluster_fold_span_guard,

   461	            read_chains, genome_fasta, strand,
   466	
   469	        # so truncated partials become one full-length candidate. Takes precedence
   470	        # over canonical-search expansion.

   474	            tss_min_reads=denovo_graph_tss_min_reads, tss_frac=denovo_graph_tss_frac,
   481

	            interval.chrom, interval.start, interval.end
   487	        )
   488	
   489	    # 4. Build GTF candidates with intron chains
   490	    gtf_candidates = []
   491	    for tx in gtf_transcripts:

   493	        # chain-cluster path). No effect when the interval is unstranded / no GTF.
   497	        tx.sort_features()

   522	    candidates: List[TranscriptCandidate] = list(gtf_candidates)
   523	    matched_group_keys: Set = set()
   524

   526	        consensus_3prime, chain = group_key

   528

   537	                matched_group_keys.add(group_key)

   556	                ref_end = rep.get("reference_end", interval.end)

   594	    # fusion candidates are not affected by this filter.   597	        collapsed = [
   598	            c
   599	            for c in collapsed
   600	            if c.source != "novel" or len(c.supporting_read_ids) >= min_novel_reads
   601	        ]
   602	        dropped = before - len(collapsed)

   604	            logger.info(
   605	                "Interval %s: dropped %d novel candidates with <%d supporting reads",
   606	                interval.region_string,
   607	                dropped,

   616	        sum(1 for c in collapsed if c.source == "novel"),
   617	        len(all_read_ids),
   618	    )

   630	    fusion_candidates: List[TranscriptCandidate],
   633	
   634	    Args:

   638	    Returns:
   639	        The same CandidateSet instance with fusion candidates appended.
   640	    """
   641	    existing_ids: Set[str] = {c.candidate_id for c in candidate_set.candidates}
   642	    for fc in fusion_candidates:
   643	        if fc.candidate_id not in existing_ids:
   644	            candidate_set.candidates.append(fc)
   645	            existing_ids.add(fc.candidate_id)
   646	
   647	    all_ids = [c.candidate_id for c in candidate_set.candidates]
   648	    if len(all_ids) != len(set(all_ids)):
   649	        raise ValueError("Duplicate candidate_ids found after merge")
   650

   665	
   666	def _wobble_collapse_novel(
   667	    novel: List[TranscriptCandidate], threshold: int, tol: int,
   668	    shadow_ratio: float = 1.0,
   674	    it and vanish. Deterministic order: (-support, -length, start, 3', end, chain).
   675	
   676	    ``shadow_ratio`` guards against merging genuine CLOSE isoforms (real
   677	    alternative donor/acceptor sites a few bp apart, e.g. NAGNAG): a candidate is
   678   681	    support and stays separate. isoquant's bulge-removal uses the ½ analogue.
   690	    # highest read support first (= consensus), then longest, then genomic
   691	    # coords + chain. This makes both the representative choice AND the greedy
   692	    # clustering deterministic regardless of the candidates' input order.
   697	        key=lambda c: (-len(c.supporting_read_ids), -c.length, c.start,
   698	                       c.three_prime_pos, c.end, tuple(c.intron_chain.introns),
   699	                       c.candidate_id),
   700	    )
   701	    reps: List[TranscriptCandidate] = []
   702	    for cand in order:
   703   704	        for rep in reps:
   705	            if (abs(rep.three_prime_pos - cand.three_prime_pos) > threshold
   706	                    or not _wobble_chain_match(rep.intron_chain, cand.intron_chain, tol)):
   707	                continue
   708	            # EXACT-chain matches always merge (superset of the disabled exact
   709	            # path). The shadow_ratio guard applies ONLY to inexact (wobbled)
   710	            # matches — it protects genuine close isoforms, not exact duplicates.
   711	            exact = rep.intron_chain == cand.intron_chain
   712	            if exact or (len(cand.supporting_read_ids)
   713	                         <= shadow_ratio * len(rep.supporting_read_ids)):
   714	                rep.supporting_read_ids.update(cand.supporting_read_ids)
   715	                merged = True
   716	                break
   717	        if not merged:
   718	            reps.append(cand)
   719	    return reps
   720	
   721	
   722	def _exact_collapse_novel(
   723	    novel: List[TranscriptCandidate], threshold: int
   724	) -> List[TranscriptCandidate]:
   725	    """Exact-chain collapse (THE default path — byte-identical when wobble is
   726	    off). Bucket novel candidates by EXACT intron chain, then within each bucket
   727	    greedily merge candidates whose 3' ends are within ``threshold`` of an
   728	    existing (longest-span) representative, unioning supporting reads."""
   729	    novel_by_chain: dict = {}
   730	    for cand in novel:
   731	        novel_by_chain.setdefault(cand.intron_chain, []).append(cand)
   732	    collapsed: List[TranscriptCandidate] = []
   733	    for chain, bucket in novel_by_chain.items():
   734	        # Sort by length desc so the longest-span candidate is the representative
   735	        # of its 3' cluster (avoiding "first-wins" arbitrariness).
   736	        bucket.sort(key=lambda c: c.length, reverse=True)
   737	        reps: List[TranscriptCandidate] = []
   738	        for cand in bucket:
   739	            merged = False
   740	            for rep in reps:
   741	                if abs(rep.three_prime_pos - cand.three_prime_pos) <= threshold:
   742	                    # rep is already at least as long (sort order). Only union the
   743	                    # supporting reads; do NOT extend rep.start/rep.end (rep.sequence
   744	                    # was built from rep's own span; coords/sequence must stay
   745	                    # consistent — #P2-3).
   746	                    rep.supporting_read_ids.update(cand.supporting_read_ids)
   747	                    merged = True
   748	                    break
   749	            if not merged:
   750	                reps.append(cand)
   751	        collapsed.extend(reps)
   752	    return collapsed
   753	
   754	
   755	def _collapse_candidates(
   756	    candidates: List[TranscriptCandidate], threshold: int, wobble_tol: int = 0,
   757	    wobble_shadow_ratio: float = 1.0,
   758	) -> List[TranscriptCandidate]:
   759	    """Collapse novel candidates with same intron chain and nearby 3' end.
   760	
   761	    The exact-chain collapse (:func:`_exact_collapse_novel`) ALWAYS runs. When
   762	    ``wobble_tol`` > 0, its reps are then wobble-collapsed
   763	    (:func:`_wobble_collapse_novel`) — so wobble-on is a STRICT SUPERSET of the
   764	    exact collapse (exact 3'-chaining fully preserved; wobble only adds inexact
   765	    shadow absorption). ``wobble_tol == 0`` is byte-identical (exact only).
   766	
   767	    Two-pass algorithm (A1):
   768	      Pass 1: bucket NOVEL candidates by exact intron chain.
   769	      Pass 2: within each chain-bucket, greedily merge candidates whose 3'
   770	              ends fall within ``threshold`` of an existing representative.
   771	    The representative is always the longest-span candidate (5' extension);
   772	    supporting read IDs are unioned.
   773	
   774	    GTF candidates are passed through unchanged (annotation-level duplicates
   775	    are preserved so ground-truth recall metrics stay interpretable).
   776	    Fusion candidates are also passed through unchanged.
   777	    """
   778	    if not candidates:
   779	        return []
   780	
   781	    gtf_passthrough: List[TranscriptCandidate] = []
   782	    fusion_candidates: List[TranscriptCandidate] = []
   783	    novel: List[TranscriptCandidate] = []
   784	
   785	    for cand in candidates:
   786	        if cand.source == "fusion":
   787	            fusion_candidates.append(cand)
   788	            continue
   789	        if cand.source == "gtf":
   790	            gtf_passthrough.append(cand)
   791	            continue
   792	        novel.append(cand)
   793	
   794	    # Exact collapse ALWAYS (byte-identical to the disabled path); wobble on top.
   795	    collapsed_novel = _exact_collapse_novel(novel, threshold)
   796	    if wobble_tol > 0:
   797	        collapsed_novel = _wobble_collapse_novel(
   798	            collapsed_novel, threshold, wobble_tol, wobble_shadow_ratio)
   799	
   800	    return gtf_passthrough + collapsed_novel + fusion_candidates

   379	    from fin.io.io_bam import BamReader
   380	
   381	    # 1. Fetch reads for interval, skip fusion reads
   382	    all_read_ids: Set[str] = set()
   383	    non_fusion_reads = []
   384	    read_sequences: dict = {}
   385	
   386	    with BamReader(bam_path) as bam:
   387	        for alignment in bam.fetch(
   388	            reference=interval.chrom, start=interval.start, end=interval.end
   389	        ):
   390	            rd = bam.alignment_to_dict(alignment)
   391	            if not rd or not rd.get("is_mapped"):
   392	                continue
   393	            # Honor strand-separated intervals. bam.fetch returns reads on BOTH
   394	            # strands overlapping the region, but each read belongs to its own
   395	            # mapped strand's interval. Without this guard an antisense interval
   396	            # that spatially contains a sense interval swallows the sense reads,
   397	            # corrupting strand assignment (a candidate emitted on the wrong
   398	            # strand can never match) and read-to-candidate quantification.
   399	            if interval.strand is not None:
   400	                read_strand = "+" if rd.get("is_forward") else "-"
   401	                if read_strand != interval.strand:
   402	                    continue
   403	            read_id = rd.get("query_name")
   404	            if read_id:
   405	                all_read_ids.add(read_id)
   406	                # Capture primary-alignment sequence only — scope is this
   407	                # interval's fetch, satisfying the per-interval read-set
   408	                # constraint. Used by R1 mappy-argmax bypass.
   409	                if not rd.get("is_secondary") and not rd.get("is_supplementary"):
   410	                    seq = rd.get("query_sequence")
   411	                    if seq:
   412	                        read_sequences.setdefault(read_id, seq)
   413	            if is_fusion_read(rd):
   414	                continue
   415	            non_fusion_reads.append(rd)
   416	
   417	    if not non_fusion_reads:
   418	        return CandidateSet(
   419	            interval=interval,
   420	            candidates=[],
   421	            read_ids=all_read_ids,
   422	            read_sequences=read_sequences,
   423	        )
   424	
   425	    # Determine strand from reads
   426	    strand = interval.strand or "+"
   427	
   428	    # NEW generation-side clustering (production default). Group reads by intron chain
   429	    # (3' ignored), collapse EXACT sub-chains, cluster wobble/cassette/containment
   430	    # keeping members; NO canonical-search shadow generation. Bypasses the legacy
   431	    # 3'+exact-chain grouping and canonical expansion entirely.
   432	    if chain_cluster:
   433	        return _chain_cluster_candidates(
   434	            interval, non_fusion_reads, all_read_ids, read_sequences,
   435	            gtf_reader, genome_fasta, strand, min_novel_reads,
   436	            wobble_bp=chain_cluster_wobble_bp,
   437	            cassette_max_exon_bp=chain_cluster_cassette_max_exon_bp,
   438	            fold_monoexon_contained=chain_cluster_fold_monoexon,
   439	            fold_span_guard=chain_cluster_fold_span_guard,
   440	        )
   441	
   442	    # 2. Group reads by 3' + intron chain → novel candidate groups.
   443	    # Optional pre-processing:
   444	    #   - canonical_search_bp>0: for each junction, scan the genome ±N bp for
   445	    #     canonical GT-AG motifs and emit the resulting chain alternatives
   446	    #     alongside the original (recovering ambiguous-GT-AG strict misses
   447	    #     where multiple valid splice sites exist within a few bp).
   448	    multi_chain_override = None
   449	
   450	    if canonical_search_bp > 0:
   451	        # Pre-extract CIGAR-derived chains once.
   452	        read_chains = []
   453	        for rd in non_fusion_reads:
   454	            cigar = rd.get("cigartuples")
   455	            ref_start = rd.get("reference_start")
   456	            if cigar is None or ref_start is None:
   457	                continue
   458	            read_chains.append((rd, extract_intron_chain(cigar, ref_start)))
   459	
   460	        multi_chain_override = expand_canonical_chain_alternatives_v2(
   461	            read_chains, genome_fasta, strand,
   462	            motif_set=parse_motifs(canonical_motifs),
   463	            search_bp=canonical_search_bp,
   464	            max_chains_per_read=max_chains_per_read,
   465	        )
   466	
   467	    if denovo_graph:
   468	        # Intron-graph assembly: group reads by their EXTENDED (assembled) chain
   469	        # so truncated partials become one full-length candidate. Takes precedence
   470	        # over canonical-search expansion.
   471	        groups = _graph_groups(
   472	            non_fusion_reads, strand, denovo_graph_tol, denovo_graph_min_edge_reads,
   473	            tss_brake=denovo_graph_tss_brake, tss_tol=denovo_graph_tss_tol,
   474	            tss_min_reads=denovo_graph_tss_min_reads, tss_frac=denovo_graph_tss_frac,
   475	        )
   476	    else:
   477	        groups = group_reads_by_three_prime_and_intron_chain(
   478	            non_fusion_reads, strand, threshold=threshold,
   479	            multi_chain_override=multi_chain_override,
   480	        )
   481	
   482	    # 3. Get GTF transcripts in region
   483	    gtf_transcripts = []
   484	    if gtf_reader is not None:
   485	        gtf_transcripts = gtf_reader.get_transcripts_in_region(
   486	            interval.chrom, interval.start, interval.end
   487	        )
   488	
   489	    # 4. Build GTF candidates with intron chains
   490	    gtf_candidates = []
   491	    for tx in gtf_transcripts:
   492	        # Strand-separated interval: skip opposite-strand annotation (mirror of the
   493	        # chain-cluster path). No effect when the interval is unstranded / no GTF.
   494	        if interval.strand is not None and tx.strand != interval.strand:
   495	            continue
   496	        chain = gtf_transcript_to_intron_chain(tx)
   497	        tx.sort_features()
   498	        # Get 3' position from GTF
   499	        if tx.strand == "+":
   500	            three_prime = tx.end
   501	        else:
   502	            three_prime = tx.start
   503	
   504	        seq = tx.get_spliced_sequence(genome_fasta)
   505	
   506	        gtf_candidates.append(
   507	            TranscriptCandidate(
   508	                candidate_id=tx.transcript_id,
   509	                intron_chain=chain,
   510	                three_prime_pos=three_prime,
   511	                sequence=seq,
   512	                source="gtf",
   513	                supporting_read_ids=set(),
   514	                chrom=interval.chrom,
   515	                strand=tx.strand,
   516	                start=tx.start,
   517	                end=tx.end,
   518	            )
   519	        )
   520	
   521	    # 5. Match novel groups vs GTF: same intron chain + 3' within threshold → merge
   522	    candidates: List[TranscriptCandidate] = list(gtf_candidates)
   523	    matched_group_keys: Set = set()
   524	
   525	    for group_key, reads in groups.items():
   526	        consensus_3prime, chain = group_key
   527	        read_ids_in_group = {rd["query_name"] for rd in reads if "query_name" in rd}
   528	
   529	        # Try to match with a GTF candidate
   530	        matched = False
   531	        for gtf_cand in gtf_candidates:
   532	            if _intron_chains_match(chain, gtf_cand.intron_chain) and _three_prime_within_threshold(
   533	                consensus_3prime, gtf_cand.three_prime_pos, threshold
   534	            ):
   535	                gtf_cand.supporting_read_ids.update(read_ids_in_group)
   536	                matched = True
   537	                matched_group_keys.add(group_key)
   538	                break
   539	
   540	        if not matched:
   541	            # Novel candidate: 5' takes longest read in the group; the spliced
   542	            # cDNA sequence is stitched from genome FASTA + the intron chain.
   543	            rep = pick_representative_read(reads)
   544	            if denovo_graph:
   545	                # Assembled candidate spans regions no single read covers -> use
   546	                # the UNION of the group's reads' extents so the genome-stitched
   547	                # spliced sequence covers the full extended intron chain.
   548	                starts = [rd.get("reference_start") for rd in reads
   549	                          if rd.get("reference_start") is not None]
   550	                ends = [rd.get("reference_end") for rd in reads
   551	                        if rd.get("reference_end") is not None]
   552	                ref_start = min(starts) if starts else interval.start
   553	                ref_end = max(ends) if ends else interval.end
   554	            else:
   555	                ref_start = rep.get("reference_start", interval.start)
   556	                ref_end = rep.get("reference_end", interval.end)
   557	
   558	            spliced_seq = _build_spliced_sequence(
   559	                genome_fasta, ref_start, ref_end, chain, strand
   560	            )
   561	            if not spliced_seq:
   562	                logger.warning(
   563	                    "Skipping novel candidate at %s:%d-%d (%s): empty spliced "
   564	                    "sequence from genome FASTA (chrom_len=%d).",
   565	                    interval.chrom,
   566	                    ref_start,
   567	                    ref_end,
   568	                    strand,
   569	                    len(genome_fasta) if genome_fasta else 0,
   570	                )
   571	                continue
   572	
   573	            candidates.append(
   574	                TranscriptCandidate(
   575	                    candidate_id=_generate_novel_id(),
   576	                    intron_chain=chain,
   577	                    three_prime_pos=consensus_3prime,
   578	                    sequence=spliced_seq,
   579	                    source="novel",
   580	                    supporting_read_ids=read_ids_in_group,
   581	                    chrom=interval.chrom,
   582	                    strand=strand,
   583	                    start=ref_start,
   584	                    end=ref_end,
   585	                )
   586	            )
   587	
   588	    # 6. Collapse: novel candidates with same intron chain + nearby 3' end
   589	    collapsed = _collapse_candidates(
   590	        candidates, threshold, wobble_tol=denovo_wobble_tol,
   591	        wobble_shadow_ratio=denovo_wobble_shadow_ratio)
   592	
   593	    # 7. (A2) Drop novel candidates below min_novel_reads threshold. GTF and
   594	    # fusion candidates are not affected by this filter.
   595	    if min_novel_reads > 1:
   596	        before = len(collapsed)
   597	        collapsed = [
   598	            c
   599	            for c in collapsed
   600	            if c.source != "novel" or len(c.supporting_read_ids) >= min_novel_reads
   601	        ]
   602	        dropped = before - len(collapsed)
   603	        if dropped:
   604	            logger.info(
   605	                "Interval %s: dropped %d novel candidates with <%d supporting reads",
   606	                interval.region_string,
   607	                dropped,
   608	                min_novel_reads,
   609	            )
   610	
   611	    logger.info(
   612	        "Interval %s: %d candidates (%d GTF, %d novel) from %d reads",
   613	        interval.region_string,
   614	        len(collapsed),
   615	        sum(1 for c in collapsed if c.source == "gtf"),
   616	        sum(1 for c in collapsed if c.source == "novel"),
   617	        len(all_read_ids),
   618	    )
   619	
   620	    return CandidateSet(
   621	        interval=interval,
   622	        candidates=collapsed,
   623	        read_ids=all_read_ids,
   624	        read_sequences=read_sequences,
   625	    )

[36m└[34m─([33m16:11:41[34m[00m on [32mdev[00m[36m ?✭[34m)[36m──> [?2004hexit[?2004l

 succeeded in 479742ms:
%                                                                               (base) [36m┌─[30m([32m/SSD/logan/dev/pyfin[30m)[36m────────────────────────────[30m([36mlogan[30m@[32mmnemosyne4:pts/17[30m)[36m─┐
[36m└[34m─([33m16:10:46[34m[00m[34m)[36m──> [?2004h(base) [36m┌─[30m([32m/SSD/logan/dev/pyfin[30m)[36m────────────────────────────[30m([36mlogan[30m@[32mmnemosyne4:pts/17[30m)[36m─┐
[36m└[34m─([33m16:10:46[34m[00m[36m ?✭[34m)[36m──> (base) [36m┌─[30m([32m/SSD/logan/dev/pyfin[30m)[36m────────────────────────────[30m([36mlogan[30m@[32mmnemosyne4:pts/17[30m)[36m─┐
[36m└[34m─([33m16:10:46[34m[00m on [32mdev[00m[36m ?✭[34m)[36m──> nl -ba fin/candidates/chain_cluster.py[?2004l
     1	"""Generation-side intron-chain clustering.
     2	
     3	Cluster-first candidate generation. Pool all reads, group by their EXACT (raw,
     4	un-snapped) intron chain, then:
     5	
     6	  1. COLLAPSE exact sub-chains  -- a chain that is a strict contiguous sub-chain of a
     7	     longer chain with IDENTICAL coordinates folds into it (reads pooled, chain gone).
     8	  2. CLUSTER the survivors      -- union-find groups the remaining distinct chains into
     9	     one cluster when related by any of:
    10	       * WOBBLE      : same intron count, every junction within ``wobble_bp``,
    11	       * CASSETTE    : K vs K-1 chains differing by one small (< ``cassette_max_exon_bp``)
    12	                       skipped exon (minimap2's small-exon-skip artefact),
    13	       * CONTAINMENT : a shorter chain that is a contiguous sub-chain of a longer one
    14	                       within ``wobble_bp`` per junction (the non-exact leftovers that
    15	                       step 1 did not fold).
    16	
    17	A cluster KEEPS all its member candidates (distinct chains + pooled reads); only exact
    18	sub-chains disappear. NO consensus snapping (coordinates are never rewritten). The
    19	cluster is the downstream unit: reads attach to a cluster instead of splitting across
    20	near-duplicates, and which members are genuine isoforms is decided later by the
    21	per-cluster computation. The caller runs with canonical_search_bp = 0 (no shadows).
    22	
    23	Mono-exon chains never join a structural cluster; each unique mono chain is a singleton.
    24	"""
    25	from __future__ import annotations
    26	
    27	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
    28	from typing import Dict, List, Set, Tuple
    29	
    30	from fin.candidates.dataclasses import IntronChain
    31	
    32	Chain = Tuple[Tuple[int, int], ...]
    33	
    34	
    35	@dataclass
    36	class GenCandidate:
    37	    """One candidate inside a cluster: a distinct intron chain + its pooled reads.
    38	
    39	    ``folded`` holds the SHADOW sub-chains folded into this candidate (each an exact
    40	    contiguous sub-chain, with its OWN reads). They are dormant provenance for the
    41	    downstream short-isoform recovery step: their truncation boundary is a proposed
    42	    TSS/polyA site and their reads are the direct endpoint evidence. ``read_ids``
    43	    already INCLUDES every shadow's reads (pooled), so shadows never add read mass."""
    44	    chain: IntronChain
    45	    read_ids: Set[str] = field(default_factory=set)
    46	    folded: List["GenCandidate"] = field(default_factory=list)
    47	
    48	    @property
    49	    def num_introns(self) -> int:
    50	        return len(self.chain.introns)
    51	
    52	
    53	@dataclass
    54	class ChainCluster:
    55	    """A structural family of candidate chains sharing their reads' assignment.
    56	
    57	    ``members`` are the distinct surviving candidates (exact sub-chains already folded
    58	    away). ``read_ids`` is the union of all members' reads. ``representative`` is the
    59	    longest member (convenience; NOT a snapped consensus -- original coordinates)."""
    60	    members: List[GenCandidate]
    61	    read_ids: Set[str] = field(default_factory=set)
    62	
    63	    @property
    64	    def representative(self) -> GenCandidate:
    65	        return max(self.members,
    66	                   key=lambda m: (m.num_introns, len(m.read_ids), m.chain.introns))
    67	
    68	
    69	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    70	# chain-relation predicates (single interval/strand: no chrom/strand bucketing)
    71	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    72	def _exact_subchain(short: Chain, long_: Chain) -> bool:
    73	    """``short`` is a strict EXACT (identical coords) contiguous sub-chain of ``long_``."""
    74	    n, m = len(long_), len(short)
    75	    if m == 0 or m >= n:
    76	        return False
    77	    return any(long_[off:off + m] == short for off in range(n - m + 1))
    78	
    79	
    80	def _subchain_offset(short: Chain, long_: Chain) -> int:
    81	    """Offset of the first exact ``short`` occurrence in ``long_`` (-1 if none). The
    82	    introns of ``long_`` outside ``[off, off+len(short))`` are the container's EXTRA
    83	    introns relative to ``short`` -- positions where ``short`` splices differently."""
    84	    n, m = len(long_), len(short)
    85	    if m == 0 or m >= n:
    86	        return -1
    87	    for off in range(n - m + 1):
    88	        if long_[off:off + m] == short:
    89	            return off
    90	    return -1
    91	
    92	
    93	def _wobble(a: Chain, b: Chain, bp: int) -> bool:
    94	    """Same intron count, every donor/acceptor within ``bp``."""
    95	    if len(a) != len(b):
    96	        return False
    97	    return all(abs(s1 - s2) <= bp and abs(e1 - e2) <= bp
    98	               for (s1, e1), (s2, e2) in zip(a, b))
    99	
   100	
   101	def _contains(short: Chain, long_: Chain, bp: int) -> bool:
   102	    """``short`` is a strictly-shorter contiguous sub-chain of ``long_`` within ``bp``."""
   103	    n, m = len(long_), len(short)
   104	    if m == 0 or m >= n:
   105	        return False
   106	    for off in range(n - m + 1):
   107	        if all(abs(short[k][0] - long_[off + k][0]) <= bp and
   108	               abs(short[k][1] - long_[off + k][1]) <= bp for k in range(m)):
   109	            return True
   110	    return False
   111	
   112	
   113	def _cassette(a: Chain, b: Chain, bp: int, max_exon_bp: int) -> bool:
   114	    """``a`` (K introns) is a cassette-skip sibling of ``b`` (K-1 introns): at one
   115	    position ``i`` the two introns ``a[i], a[i+1]`` replace ``b[i]``'s span (outer
   116	    donor/acceptor within bp), the skipped exon between them is < ``max_exon_bp``, and
   117	    every other intron matches within bp."""
   118	    if max_exon_bp <= 0 or len(a) != len(b) + 1:
   119	        return False
   120	    for i in range(len(a) - 1):
   121	        if any(abs(a[k][0] - b[k][0]) > bp or abs(a[k][1] - b[k][1]) > bp
   122	               for k in range(i)):
   123	            continue
   124	        if abs(a[i][0] - b[i][0]) > bp or abs(a[i + 1][1] - b[i][1]) > bp:
   125	            continue
   126	        rest = len(b) - i - 1
   127	        if any(abs(a[i + 2 + k][0] - b[i + 1 + k][0]) > bp or
   128	               abs(a[i + 2 + k][1] - b[i + 1 + k][1]) > bp for k in range(rest)):
   129	            continue
   130	        cas = a[i + 1][0] - a[i][1]
   131	        if 0 < cas < max_exon_bp:
   132	            return True
   133	    return False
   134	
   135	
   136	def _related(a: Chain, b: Chain, wobble_bp: int, cassette_max_exon_bp: int) -> bool:
   137	    """True iff ``a`` and ``b`` should share a cluster (wobble / cassette / containment)."""
   138	    if _wobble(a, b, wobble_bp):
   139	        return True
   140	    lo, hi = (a, b) if len(a) < len(b) else (b, a)
   141	    if len(lo) < len(hi):
   142	        if _cassette(hi, lo, wobble_bp, cassette_max_exon_bp):
   143	            return True
   144	        if _contains(lo, hi, wobble_bp):
   145	            return True
   146	    return False
   147	
   148	
   149	def _monoexon_in_exon(s: int, e: int, chain: Chain, lo: int, hi: int) -> bool:
   150	    """``[s, e)`` (a single-exon read) lies wholly inside ONE exon of a multi-exon
   151	    candidate whose exonic footprint is ``[lo, hi)`` and whose introns are ``chain``
   152	    (each ``[d, a)`` half-open). True iff the read is within the footprint AND overlaps
   153	    no intron -- i.e. it is a fragment of that candidate's exonic sequence, not an
   154	    intronic feature (a different gene) and not something that crosses a junction."""
   155	    if s < lo or e > hi:
   156	        return False
   157	    for d, a in chain:                       # overlap of [s,e) and intron [d,a)
   158	        if s < a and d < e:
   159	            return False
   160	    return True
   161	
   162	
   163	def cluster_read_chains(
   164	    read_chains: List[Tuple[Dict, IntronChain]],
   165	    *,
   166	    wobble_bp: int = 6,
   167	    cassette_max_exon_bp: int = 70,
   168	    fold_monoexon_contained: bool = False,
   169	    fold_span_guard: bool = False,
   170	) -> List[ChainCluster]:
   171	    """Build generation-side clusters from per-read chains (NO snap, 3' ignored).
   172	
   173	    Args:
   174	        read_chains: per-read ``(read_dict, IntronChain)`` (read_dict needs
   175	            "query_name"; CIGAR-derived chains, used as-is).
   176	        wobble_bp: per-junction tolerance for wobble / cassette / containment joins.
   177	        cassette_max_exon_bp: max skipped-exon length for a cassette join (0 off).
   178	        fold_monoexon_contained: when True, a single-exon (chain-less) read whose
   179	            aligned span lies wholly inside one exon of a multi-exon candidate is folded
   180	            into that candidate (its read joins the candidate's support) instead of
   181	            emitting a standalone mono candidate. This absorbs 5'/3' degradation
   182	            fragments of a spliced transcript. Reads inside an INTRON (a different gene)
   183	            or not contained in any multi-exon candidate stay as mono candidates. Off by
   184	            default (byte-identical legacy behaviour).
   185	        fold_span_guard: when True, a read only folds into an exact-sub-chain container
   186	            if its aligned span does NOT run exonically across one of the container's
   187	            EXTRA introns (introns present in the container but absent from the read's
   188	            chain). A read that spans such an intron contradicts it (retained-intron or
   189	            alternative isoform) and is kept as its own candidate instead of being
   190	            absorbed. Off by default (byte-identical legacy behaviour).
   191	
   192	    Returns:
   193	        List of :class:`ChainCluster`. EVERY exact contiguous sub-chain is folded
   194	        (unconditionally, no read-support guard) into its longest exact container --
   195	        generation does pure structural collapse and does NOT decide whether a folded
   196	        sub-chain is a real short isoform; that judgement is a SEPARATE downstream
   197	        recovery step (e.g. read-end pileup), not done here. The survivors are unioned
   198	        into clusters by wobble/cassette/containment; each unique mono chain is its own
   199	        singleton cluster.
   200	    """
   201	    # 1. group reads by EXACT chain (no 3', no snap).
   202	    by_chain: Dict[Chain, Set[str]] = {}
   203	    for rd, chain in read_chains:
   204	        rid = rd.get("query_name")
   205	        if rid is None:
   206	            continue
   207	        by_chain.setdefault(tuple(chain.introns), set()).add(rid)
   208	
   209	    # Read spans (0-based [start, end)) -- needed by the span-guarded fold and the
   210	    # mono-exon fold. Built once, only when a span-aware step is enabled.
   211	    read_span: Dict[str, Tuple[int, int]] = {}
   212	    if fold_span_guard or fold_monoexon_contained:
   213	        for rd, _chain in read_chains:
   214	            rid = rd.get("query_name")
   215	            rs = rd.get("reference_start")
   216	            re_ = rd.get("reference_end")
   217	            if rid is not None and rs is not None and re_ is not None:
   218	                read_span[rid] = (rs, re_)
   219	
   220	    # 2. collapse every exact contiguous sub-chain into its longest exact container.
   221	    #    Default: UNCONDITIONAL (no read-support guard). With fold_span_guard, a read
   222	    #    folds ONLY if its aligned span does not run exonically across one of the
   223	    #    container's EXTRA introns (retained-intron / alternative isoform reads are kept
   224	    #    as their own candidate instead of being absorbed). Whether a *consistent*
   225	    #    folded sub-chain is a genuine short isoform is still a SEPARATE downstream
   226	    #    recovery step (read-end pileup), never guessed here from read counts.
   227	    chains_sorted = sorted(by_chain, key=lambda c: (-len(c), -len(by_chain[c]), c))
   228	    kept: List[Chain] = []
   229	    reads: Dict[Chain, Set[str]] = {}
   230	    folded_of: Dict[Chain, List[Tuple[Chain, Set[str]]]] = {}  # container -> shadows
   231	    for c in chains_sorted:
   232	        container = next((k for k in kept if _exact_subchain(c, k)), None)
   233	        if container is None:
   234	            kept.append(c)
   235	            reads[c] = set(by_chain[c])
   236	            continue
   237	        if not fold_span_guard:
   238	            reads[container] |= by_chain[c]
   239	            folded_of.setdefault(container, []).append((c, set(by_chain[c])))
   240	            continue
   241	        # span guard: split c's reads into those consistent with the container (fold)
   242	        # and those exonic across one of the container's extra introns (keep as c).
   243	        off = _subchain_offset(c, container)
   244	        extra = container[:off] + container[off + len(c):]
   245	        fold_r: Set[str] = set()
   246	        keep_r: Set[str] = set()
   247	        for rid in by_chain[c]:
   248	            sp = read_span.get(rid)
   249	            if sp is not None and any(sp[0] <= d and sp[1] >= a for d, a in extra):
   250	                keep_r.add(rid)            # exonic across an extra intron -> distinct
   251	            else:
   252	                fold_r.add(rid)
   253	        if fold_r:
   254	            reads[container] |= fold_r
   255	            folded_of.setdefault(container, []).append((c, fold_r))
   256	        if keep_r:
   257	            kept.append(c)
   258	            reads[c] = keep_r
   259	
   260	    # 3. union-find the survivors by wobble / cassette / containment (multi-exon only).
   261	    multi = [c for c in kept if len(c) >= 1]
   262	    mono = [c for c in kept if len(c) == 0]
   263	
   264	    # 3b. (optional) fold single-exon reads that are wholly inside a multi-exon
   265	    #     candidate's exon into that candidate -- 5'/3' degradation fragments of a
   266	    #     spliced transcript, otherwise emitted as standalone mono FPs. Per-read (the
   267	    #     mono chain pools every chain-less read), deterministic container choice.
   268	    if fold_monoexon_contained and mono and multi:
   269	        multi_span: Dict[Chain, Tuple[int, int]] = {}
   270	        for c in multi:
   271	            pts = [read_span[r] for r in reads[c] if r in read_span]
   272	            if pts:
   273	                multi_span[c] = (min(p[0] for p in pts), max(p[1] for p in pts))
   274	        containers = [c for c in multi if c in multi_span]
   275	        for mc in mono:
   276	            remaining: Set[str] = set()
   277	            for rid in reads[mc]:
   278	                sp = read_span.get(rid)
   279	                if sp is None:
   280	                    remaining.add(rid)
   281	                    continue
   282	                s, e = sp
   283	                hits = [c for c in containers
   284	                        if _monoexon_in_exon(s, e, c, *multi_span[c])]
   285	                if not hits:
   286	                    remaining.add(rid)
   287	                    continue
   288	                best = max(hits, key=lambda c: (len(reads[c]), len(c), c))
   289	                reads[best].add(rid)
   290	                folded_of.setdefault(best, []).append(((), {rid}))
   291	            reads[mc] = remaining
   292	        mono = [c for c in mono if reads[c]]
   293	
   294	    parent = {c: c for c in multi}
   295	
   296	    def find(x: Chain) -> Chain:
   297	        while parent[x] != x:
   298	            parent[x] = parent[parent[x]]
   299	            x = parent[x]
   300	        return x
   301	
   302	    for i in range(len(multi)):
   303	        for j in range(i + 1, len(multi)):
   304	            a, b = multi[i], multi[j]
   305	            if find(a) != find(b) and _related(a, b, wobble_bp, cassette_max_exon_bp):
   306	                parent[find(a)] = find(b)
   307	
   308	    comps: Dict[Chain, List[Chain]] = {}
   309	    for c in multi:
   310	        comps.setdefault(find(c), []).append(c)
   311	
   312	    def _mk(c: Chain) -> GenCandidate:
   313	        """Member candidate with its folded exact-sub-chain shadows attached."""
   314	        return GenCandidate(
   315	            IntronChain(introns=c), set(reads[c]),
   316	            folded=[GenCandidate(IntronChain(introns=fc), set(fr))
   317	                    for fc, fr in folded_of.get(c, [])])
   318	
   319	    clusters: List[ChainCluster] = []
   320	    for comp in comps.values():
   321	        members = [_mk(c) for c in comp]
   322	        union: Set[str] = set()
   323	        for c in comp:
   324	            union |= reads[c]
   325	        clusters.append(ChainCluster(members=members, read_ids=union))
   326	
   327	    for c in mono:  # mono singletons
   328	        clusters.append(ChainCluster(members=[_mk(c)], read_ids=set(reads[c])))
   329	
   330	    return clusters
%                                                                               (base) [36m┌─[30m([32m/SSD/logan/dev/pyfin[30m)[36m────────────────────────────[30m([36mlogan[30m@[32mmnemosyne4:pts/17[30m)[36m─┐
[36m└[34m─([33m16:11:25[34m[00m on [32mdev[00m[36m ?✭[34m)[36m──> [?2004hexit[?2004l

 succeeded in 479763ms:
%                                                                               (base) [36m┌─[30m([32m/SSD/logan/dev/pyfin[30m)[36m────────────────────────────[30m([36mlogan[30m@[32mmnemosyne4:pts/18[30m)[36m─┐
[36m└[34m─([33m16:10:46[34m[00m[34m)[36m──> [?2004h(base) [36m┌─[30m([32m/SSD/logan/dev/pyfin[30m)[36m────────────────────────────[30m([36mlogan[30m@[32mmnemosyne4:pts/18[30m)[36m─┐
[36m└[34m─([33m16:10:46[34m[00m on [32mdev[00m[34m)[36m──> (base) [36m┌─[30m([32m/SSD/logan/dev/pyfin[30m)[36m────────────────────────────[30m([36mlogan[30m@[32mmnemosyne4:pts/18[30m)[36m─┐
[36m└[34m─([33m16:10:46[34m[00m on [32mdev[00m[36m ?✭[34m)[36m──> nl -ba fin/candidates/intron_chains.py[?2004l
     1	"""Intron chain extraction and read grouping for candidate discovery."""
     2	
     3	from __future__ import annotations
     4	
     5	import logging
     6	from collections import Counter, defaultdict
     7	from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
     8	
     9	from fin.candidates.dataclasses import IntronChain
    10	from fin.io.interval_manager import extract_three_prime_pos
    11	
    12	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    13	
    14	# pysam CIGAR operation codes
    15	_CIGAR_N = 3  # skipped region (intron)
    16	
    17	
    18	def extract_intron_chain(
    19	    cigartuples: List[Tuple[int, int]], reference_start: int
    20	) -> IntronChain:
    21	    """Extract intron chain from CIGAR tuples.
    22	
    23	    Walks the CIGAR string collecting N (skip) operations as introns.
    24	
    25	    Args:
    26	        cigartuples: List of (operation, length) from pysam alignment.
    27	        reference_start: 0-based reference start position.
    28	
    29	    Returns:
    30	        IntronChain with sorted intron intervals.
    31	    """
    32	    introns = []
    33	    ref_pos = reference_start
    34	
    35	    for op, length in cigartuples:
    36	        if op == _CIGAR_N:
    37	            introns.append((ref_pos, ref_pos + length))
    38	            ref_pos += length
    39	        elif op in (0, 2, 7, 8):  # M, D, =, X consume reference
    40	            ref_pos += length
    41	        # S(4), I(1), H(5), P(6) do not consume reference
    42	
    43	    return IntronChain(introns=tuple(introns))

   396   399	def expand_canonical_chain_alternatives_v2(

   408

   410	    legacy donor/acceptor-independent Cartesian bug: per-junction alternatives
   411	    are enumerated as PAIRED (s', e') candidates via
   412

   426	        search_bp: Window radius (bp) around each junction. 0 disables.

	        # Quick size check before exploding the Cartesian product.

   474	            key = tuple(combo)

   485	
   486	def group_reads_by_three_prime_and_intron_chain(
   492	) -> Dict[Tuple[int, IntronChain], List[Dict]]:
   493	    """Group reads by clustered 3' end position and intron chain.
    1. Extract 3' position and intron chain per read.
   496	    2. Sort by 3' position.
   497	    3. Cluster 3' positions within *threshold* bp.
   498	    4. Sub-group each cluster by intron chain.
   499	    5. Use consensus (median) 3' position per cluster.
   500	
   501	    Args:
   502	        read_dicts: List of alignment dicts from BamReader.alignment_to_dict().
   503	        strand: '+' or '-'.
   504	        threshold: Maximum distance between 3' ends in a cluster.
   505	        chain_override: Optional mapping ``query_name -> IntronChain`` that
   506	            replaces the CIGAR-derived chain (used for junction-snap output).
   507	        multi_chain_override: Optional mapping ``query_name -> [IntronChain, ...]``
   508	            that produces multiple grouping entries per read (used for the
   509	            canonical-motif alternative expansion). Takes precedence over
   510	            ``chain_override`` when both are provided for the same read.
   511	
   512	    Returns:
   513	        Dict mapping (consensus_3prime, IntronChain) -> list of read dicts.
   514	    """
   515	    # Annotate each read with its 3' pos and intron chain
   516	    annotated: List[Tuple[int, IntronChain, Dict]] = []
   517	    for rd in read_dicts:
   518	        three_prime = extract_three_prime_pos(rd)
   519	        if three_prime is None:
   520	            continue
   521	        cigar = rd.get("cigartuples")
   522	        ref_start = rd.get("reference_start")
   523	        if cigar is None or ref_start is None:
   524	            continue
   525	        qname = rd.get("query_name")
   526	        chains_for_this_read: Optional[List[IntronChain]] = None
   527	        if multi_chain_override is not None and qname is not None \
   528	                and qname in multi_chain_override:
   529	            chains_for_this_read = multi_chain_override[qname]
   530	        elif chain_override is not None and qname is not None \
   531	                and qname in chain_override:
   532	            chains_for_this_read = [chain_override[qname]]
   533	        if chains_for_this_read is None:
   534	            chains_for_this_read = [extract_intron_chain(cigar, ref_start)]
   535	        for chain in chains_for_this_read:
   536	            annotated.append((three_prime, chain, rd))
   537	
   538	    if not annotated:
   539	        return {}
   540	
   541	    # Sort by 3' position
   542	    annotated.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])
   543	
   544	    # Cluster by 3' proximity
   545	    clusters: List[List[Tuple[int, IntronChain, Dict]]] = []
   546	    current_cluster: List[Tuple[int, IntronChain, Dict]] = [annotated[0]]
   547	
   548	    for item in annotated[1:]:
   549	        if item[0] - current_cluster[-1][0] <= threshold:
   550	            current_cluster.append(item)
   551	        else:
   552	            clusters.append(current_cluster)
   553	            current_cluster = [item]
   554	    clusters.append(current_cluster)
   555	
   556	    # Sub-group each cluster by intron chain, compute consensus 3'
   557	    groups: Dict[Tuple[int, IntronChain], List[Dict]] = {}
   558	    for cluster in clusters:
   559	        # Consensus 3' = median of cluster positions
   560	        positions = [x[0] for x in cluster]
   561	        consensus_3prime = sorted(positions)[len(positions) // 2]
   562	
   563	        by_chain: Dict[IntronChain, List[Dict]] = defaultdict(list)
   564	        for _, chain, rd in cluster:
   565	            by_chain[chain].append(rd)
   566	
   567	        for chain, reads in by_chain.items():
   568	            groups[(consensus_3prime, chain)] = reads
   569	
   570	    return groups
   571	
   572	
   573	def pick_representative_read(read_dicts: List[Dict]) -> Dict:
   574	    """Pick the longest read as the representative for a group.
   575	
   576	    Args:
   577	        read_dicts: List of alignment dicts.
   578	
   579	    Returns:
   580	        The read dict with the largest query_length.
   581	    """
   582	    return max(read_dicts, key=lambda rd: rd.get("query_length", 0))

     1	"""Data classes for transcript candidate discovery."""
     2	
     3	from __future__ import annotations
     4	
     5	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
     6	from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Set, Tuple
     7	
     8	from fin.io.interval_manager import GenomicInterval
     9	
    10	
    11	@dataclass(frozen=True)
    12	class IntronChain:
    13	    """Hashable representation of a transcript's intron structure.
    14	
    15	    Each intron is a (start, end) tuple in genomic coordinates.
    16	    """
    17	
    18	    introns: Tuple[Tuple[int, int], ...]
    19	
    20	    @property
    21	    def num_introns(self) -> int:
    22	        return len(self.introns)
    23	
    24	    @property
    25	    def is_single_exon(self) -> bool:
    26	        return len(self.introns) == 0
    27	
    28	
    29	@dataclass
    30	class TranscriptCandidate:
    31	    """A candidate transcript (from GTF annotation, novel discovery, or fusion).
    32	
    33	    Required fields (positional):
    34	        candidate_id, intron_chain, three_prime_pos, sequence, source,
    35	        supporting_read_ids, chrom, strand, start, end.
    36	
    37	    Optional fusion fields (populated only when source == "fusion"):
    38	        fusion_junction: Tuple of (left_genomic_pos, right_genomic_pos) marking
    39	            where the two parental transcripts are joined.
    40	        breakpoint_left: (chrom, pos, strand) for the left fusion partner.
    41	        breakpoint_right: (chrom, pos, strand) for the right fusion partner.
    42	    """
    43	
    44	    candidate_id: str
    45	    intron_chain: IntronChain
    46	    three_prime_pos: int
    47	    sequence: str
    48	    source: str  # "gtf", "novel", or "fusion"
    49	    supporting_read_ids: Set[str]
    50	    chrom: str
    51	    strand: str
    52	    start: int
    53	    end: int
    54	
    55	    # Fusion-specific (populated only when source == "fusion")
    56	    fusion_junction: Optional[Tuple[int, int]] = None
    57	    breakpoint_left: Optional[Tuple[str, int, str]] = None   # (chrom, pos, strand)
    58	    breakpoint_right: Optional[Tuple[str, int, str]] = None
    59	
    60	    @property
    61	    def length(self) -> int:
    62	        # Fusion candidates set start=posA on chromA and end=posB on chromB.
    63	        # Subtracting them is meaningless (and can be negative) so we fall
    64	        # back to the assembled sequence length, which is the only thing
    65	        # downstream code actually uses for fusion candidates.
    66	        if self.source == "fusion":
    67	            return len(self.sequence) if self.sequence else 0
    68	        return self.end - self.start
    69	
    70	    @property
    71	    def num_supporting_reads(self) -> int:
    72	        return len(self.supporting_read_ids)
    73	
    74	
    75	@dataclass
    76	class CandidateSet:
    77	    """Collection of transcript candidates for a genomic interval."""
    78	
    79	    interval: GenomicInterval
    80	    candidates: List[TranscriptCandidate]
    81	    read_ids: Set[str]
    82	    # read_id -> primary-alignment query_sequence, scoped to reads fetched
    83	    # within this interval. Populated by discover_candidates(); used by R1
    84	    # ablation (mappy argmax bypass) and any path that needs read FASTA.
    85	    read_sequences: Dict[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)
    86	    # Generation-cluster grouping for quant_mode="cluster": each inner list holds
    87	    # the candidate_ids that belong to one generation cluster. Ids (not indices)
    88	    # survive post-discovery gate drops/reorders (canonical/dominance/fusion), which
    89	    # would invalidate positional indices. Populated only by the chain-cluster
    90	    # discovery path; None means "not chain-cluster mode" (the quant-cluster dispatch
    91	    # then treats the whole candidate list as one unscoped cluster).
    92	    clusters: Optional[List[List[str]]] = None
    93	    # 5'-TSS short-isoform recovery provenance (chain-cluster path only). Maps a
    94	    # container candidate_id to the SHADOW sub-chains folded into it during
    95	    # generation: each shadow is (intron_chain_tuple, sorted_read_id_tuple). The
    96	    # quant-cluster recovery step re-tests these folded truncations against the
    97	    # container's read-5'-end pileup and re-activates real TSS peaks as short
    98	    # isoforms. None means "not chain-cluster mode" (no recovery).
    99	    shadows: Optional[
   100	        Dict[str, List[Tuple[Tuple[Tuple[int, int], ...], Tuple[str, ...]]]]
   101	    ] = None
   102	    # read_id -> (genomic reference_start, reference_end) for reads fetched in this
   103	    # interval. Populated by the chain-cluster discovery path; used by the post-EM
   104	    # mono-exon resolution (quant_mode="m2_em", mono_resolve_post_em) to test whether a
   105	    # single-exon read's genomic span lies inside a surviving multi-exon candidate's
   106	    # exon. None means "not chain-cluster mode" (mono-resolution no-ops).
   107	    read_spans: Optional[Dict[str, Tuple[int, int]]] = None
   108	
   109	    @property
   110	    def num_candidates(self) -> int:
   111	        return len(self.candidates)
   112	
   113	    def get_candidate(self, candidate_id: str) -> Optional[TranscriptCandidate]:
   114	        for c in self.candidates:
   115	            if c.candidate_id == candidate_id:
   116	                return c
   117	        return None
   118	
   119	    def candidate_ids(self) -> List[str]:
   120	        return [c.candidate_id for c in self.candidates]

     1	"""Pipeline configuration."""
     2	
     3	from __future__ import annotations
     4	
     5	from dataclasses import dataclass
     6	from pathlib import Path
     7	from typing import Literal, Optional
     8	
     9	
    10	@dataclass
    11	class PipelineConfig:
    12	    """Configuration for the full pyfin pipeline."""
    13	
    14	    # Input files
    15	    bam_path: str
    16	    gtf_path: Optional[str] = None
    17	    genome_fasta_path: str = ""
    18	    fastq_path: str = ""
    19	    signal_path: str = ""  # SLOW5/BLOW5 file
    20	    signal_format: str = "slow5"  # "pod5" or "slow5"
    21	
    22	    # Working directory
    23	    work_dir: str = "./pyfin_work"
    24	
    25	    # Candidate discovery
    26	    three_prime_threshold: int = 24
    27	    max_gap: int = 0
    28	    min_novel_reads: int = 1          # A2: min supporting reads for a novel candidate (after collapsing)
    29	    # NEW generation-side clustering (PRODUCTION DEFAULT). Group reads by intron chain
    30	    # (3' ignored), collapse EXACT sub-chains, cluster wobble/cassette/containment
    31	    # keeping members; NO canonical-search shadow generation. Replaces the legacy
    32	    # 3'+exact-chain grouping + canonical expansion. On p00 (no gates, candidates = the
    33	    # result) this cut raw candidates 175k->37k, c -73%, j -88%, structural precision
    34	    # 10.1%->26.4% (2.6x), at a ~20% distinct-truth recall cost that is 92% attributable
    35	    # to the exact sub-chain collapse (recoverable later by guarding the collapse).
    36	    chain_cluster_discovery: bool = True
    37	    chain_cluster_wobble_bp: int = 6
    38	    chain_cluster_cassette_max_exon_bp: int = 70
    39	    chain_cluster_fold_monoexon: bool = True  # fold single-exon reads wholly inside a multi-exon candidate's exon into that candidate (5'/3' degradation fragments) instead of emitting standalone mono candidates; intronic/uncontained mono reads stay separate. PRODUCTION DEFAULT (p00 m2_em: out 9581->9181, `=` 6323->6414, Pr 66.0->69.9 -- de-fragmentation both-axes win). --no- reverts to legacy standalone mono candidates.
    40	    chain_cluster_fold_span_guard: bool = True  # only fold a read into an exact-sub-chain container if its aligned span does NOT run exonically across one of the container's EXTRA introns; a read spanning such an intron (retained-intron / alternative isoform) is kept as its own candidate instead of being absorbed. PRODUCTION DEFAULT. --no- reverts to unconditional exact-sub-chain fold.
    41	    # Post-EM mono-exon resolution (quant_mode="m2_em" only). When True, single-exon
    42	    # reads are NOT folded into multi-exon candidates at generation (chain_cluster_fold_
    43	    # monoexon is forced off); instead, AFTER the multi-exon EM + structural gates, each
    44	    # surviving mono candidate's reads are re-resolved against the SURVIVING multi-exon
    45	    # candidates by strict strand-aware exonic containment: a read wholly inside one
    46	    # exon of exactly one surviving multi folds into it; inside several -> folds into the
    47	    # highest-EM-abundance one; uncovered reads stay on the mono candidate, which is kept
    48	    # only if it retains >= mono_resolve_min_reads (else dropped). Fragment reads follow
    49	    # inferred multi-isoform abundance instead of a static generation-time container.
    50	    # Default OFF (byte-identical). Codex-vetted; validate on p00 before any default flip.
    51	    mono_resolve_post_em: bool = False
    52	    mono_resolve_min_reads: int = 2   # uncovered reads a mono candidate must retain to survive
    53	    mono_resolve_slop_bp: int = 10    # terminal boundary slop for the exonic-containment test
    54	    min_abundance: float = 0.0        # A4: drop quantified NOVEL transcripts with abundance < this. Field default stays 0 (programmatic/quantify/ablation callers unchanged); the `fin` CLI defaults it to 3 (NOVEL only).
    55	    floor_gtf_abundance: bool = False  # A4: when True the GTF floor is raised to max(min_gtf_abundance, min_abundance) (i.e. up to the NOVEL floor, never below the explicit GTF floor); default False = GTF uses its own lighter min_gtf_abundance. The `fin` CLI exposes this as --floor-gtf-abundance (OFF). Fusion is always exempt. Ablation path is separate and keeps GTF exempt regardless.
    56	    min_gtf_abundance: float = 0.0    # A4: own (lighter) abundance floor for GTF transcripts, on soft EM abundance. Field default stays 0 (programmatic/quantify/ablation callers unchanged); the `fin` CLI defaults it to 1, so a GTF candidate whose EM soft-mass is below 1 read is dropped (avoids echoing annotation as a copy-tool). When floor_gtf_abundance is set the effective GTF floor becomes max(min_gtf_abundance, min_abundance). Fusion always exempt.
    57	    # Minimum isoform fraction (locus-relative abundance) FILTER. Drop a NOVEL
    58	    # multi-exon transcript whose abundance is below this fraction of the
    59	    # dominant OVERLAPPING novel isoform at its locus. This is the standard
    60	    # Cufflinks --min-isoform-fraction (-F, default 0.10) / StringTie -f
    61	    # (default 0.01) minor-isoform suppression heuristic: the low-fraction tail
    62	    # of a locus is enriched for incompletely-spliced pre-mRNA, RT/template-
    63	    # switching artifacts, and assembly noise rather than genuine isoforms.
    64	    # Applied as a post-quant candidate FILTER (never inside EM/assignment);
    65	    # GTF-passthrough, fusion, and single-exon candidates are EXEMPT. Default
